⏺ I've read the whole repo. It's a take-home exercise from Cadence
  (cadencerpm/preop-exercise) and nothing has been changed yet: the git tree is
  clean on main.

  The task

  You implement triage_submission(submission, model=...) in core.py
  (preop-exercise/core.py). It takes one pre-op patient package and returns:
  - decision: READY, NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP or NOT_CLEARED
  - issues[]: each has a category from 5 fixed values, plus evidence, whose
    format is yours to design
  - explanation

  The README says they grade on engineering judgment, not on reaching 100%.

  The policy (in BASELINE_SYSTEM_PROMPT)

  1. Documentation: an H&P within 30 days of the procedure, and a signed
     surgical consent.
  2. Testing: LOW or MODERATE risk needs a CBC within 30 days. HIGH risk needs a
     CBC and a CMP, each within 14 days. Only the most recent result counts.
  3. Anticoagulation: if the patient is on an anticoagulant, there must be a
     clear peri-op plan.
  4. Acute exclusions: systolic ≥180, diastolic ≥110 or temperature >100.4 °F,
     using the most recent vital, gives NOT_CLEARED.
  5. Missing data needed to check a rule gives NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP.

  Files

  File: core.py (preop-exercise/core.py)
  Role: Policy prompt, Pydantic schemas, and the naive baseline: a single
  responses.create call
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: run_baseline.py (preop-exercise/run_baseline.py)
  Role: Runs each case one after another and writes data/baseline_outputs.jsonl
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: run_evals.py (preop-exercise/run_evals.py)
  Role: Scores 3 equally weighted metrics: schema valid, decision match, exact
  category-set match. It also has a determinism mode that runs one case 10
  times.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: view_report.py (preop-exercise/view_report.py)
  Role: Terminal viewer for the eval report
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: tests/test_triage_submission.py
  (preop-exercise/tests/test_triage_submission.py)
  Role: 3 tests that mock OpenAI
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: data/patients_sample_50.jsonl
  Role: 50 labelled cases, each with a written rationale: 17 READY, 25
  NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP, 8 NOT_CLEARED

  Problems in the baseline

  The README says these are deliberate.
  - Loose output schema: strict: False, and the Pydantic schema isn't
    strict-compatible (additionalProperties and all-required are missing).
  - Nothing pinned for repeatability: no temperature or seed is set, so the
    determinism check will be weak.
  - No resilience: a new client is created on every call, with no timeout,
    retries or error handling.
  - Slow runs: cases run one at a time, with no concurrency.
  - Unstructured evidence: it's a single free-form string.
  - Model does the arithmetic: the LLM works out every date difference, unit
    conversion and "most recent" comparison itself, which is where it's likely
    to make mistakes.

  How the labels work

  Read from the 50 rationales, these are where the difficulty lies:
  - Vitals in notes: the most recent vital is often in a nursing note, not in
    the structured vitals. Notes can be in °C and include rechecks (e.g. 189/112
    rechecked to 137/78 means not held). Exactly 110 diastolic counts as an
    exclusion.
  - Labs: lab codes vary (CBC, LAB-CBC, …). Results marked entered-in-error are
    skipped. A BMP is not a CMP.
  - Date windows: they are inclusive, so exactly 30 days passes and 31 fails.
    HIGH risk has the 14-day limit.
  - H&P: document titles are messy (e.g. "History and Pyhsical"). An old
    establish-care visit doesn't count as a pre-op H&P.
  - Consent: it must be signed by the patient (not pending, not printed only,
    not an unfinished portal request), and it must be for this procedure. A
    newer signed consent replaces an earlier pending one.
  - Anticoagulation:
    - The medication list can be incomplete; notes reveal Xarelto, Eliquis or
      Coumadin.
    - Inactive warfarin needs no plan.
    - Aspirin and clopidogrel are antiplatelets, so they never need a plan.
    - A plan needs both a hold and a resume.
    - A plan written for a drug the patient has since switched from doesn't
      count.
  - When MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA applies:
    - No temperature (or no vitals at all) anywhere in the chart.
    - It's unclear whether the patient is on an anticoagulant. This takes this
      category, not ANTICOAGULATION_MANAGEMENT.
    - No procedure date or risk level. Then only this category is reported,
      because the window checks can't run.
  - NOT_CLEARED cases still list the other failing categories (e.g.
    ACUTE_SAFETY_EXCLUSION plus REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION). Since the category set
    must match exactly, this matters.

  Practical notes

  - OPENAI_API_KEY isn't set in this shell. make baseline, make evals and make
    determinism all need it; make evals also calls the OpenAI Evals API, not
    just local scoring.
  - uv 0.10.6 is installed.
  - The current tests assert strict is False and that the prompt matches
    BASELINE_SYSTEM_PROMPT exactly, so they'll need updating once the
    implementation changes.

  To start, I'd suggest a hybrid design. The LLM would only pull facts out of
  the free text (vitals in notes, whether consent was signed, which
  anticoagulant and what the plan says, whether a document is a pre-op H&P).
  Plain Python would then apply the policy rules: the date windows, thresholds,
  unit conversion and which categories to report. That keeps the decisions
  deterministic and checkable, and gives the evidence a clear structure.

⏺ I created preop-exercise/.env with an empty key line for you to fill in:

  OPENAI_API_KEY=sk-...

  - Gitignored: git check-ignore confirms the existing .gitignore rule covers
    it, so it won't be committed. I also made it readable only by you (chmod
    600).
  - Makefile change: the scripts don't read .env on their own, so I added three
    lines to the top of the Makefile (preop-exercise/Makefile) that load it and
    pass its values to every make target. A test in a scratch folder showed the
    key gets through. If .env is missing, make still runs normally.

  Two things to know:
  - No quotes around the key. make reads the line literally, so
    OPENAI_API_KEY="sk-..." would pass the quote marks as part of the key.
  - An empty line blocks a shell key. While the line in .env is blank, it
    overrides any OPENAI_API_KEY you export in your shell, and the targets get
    an empty key. Fill it in, or delete the line if you'd rather use the shell.

  make test mocks OpenAI and doesn't need the key. make baseline, make evals and
  make determinism all do.

⏺ The as-is baseline on gpt-5.6-luna scores 97.33%. Only 3 of 50 cases fail, all
  on edge cases about which issue categories to report.

  Key exposure: your key had been pasted across three lines of .env. When I
  first loaded the file, the shell printed two of those lines as error messages,
  so roughly the last 100 characters of the key now appear in this
  conversation. I fixed .env so the key is one line, and the API call works. If
  you want to be safe, rotate the key.

  How scoring works

  run_baseline.py only runs the model. Scoring is in _local_metrics_for_row in
  run_evals.py (preop-exercise/run_evals.py#L174). Each case gets three
  pass/fail checks, weighted equally:
  - Schema valid: the output has a valid decision and an issues[] list where
    each issue has a valid category. Extra fields are allowed.
  - Decision: must match the label exactly.
  - Categories: compares sorted(set(label.categories)) with
    sorted({issue.category for issue in issues}). Both sides are reduced to
    unique values first.

  So two missing documents count as one REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION. No label repeats
  a category, and you can output two issues with the same category (one per
  missing document) without penalty. The match is exact, though: one extra or
  missing category fails that check for the case, with no partial credit.

  The final score is the average over cases. One quirk: if a READY case errors
  out, it still passes the category check, because an empty result equals the
  empty label. make evals also sends the rows to OpenAI Evals, but the score it
  reports is the local one.

  Data inspection (no LLM calls)

  How decisions and categories relate in the labels:
  - READY ⇔ no categories, and NOT_CLEARED ⇔ ACUTE_SAFETY_EXCLUSION is present.
    Both hold in all 50 cases.
  - No case has more than 2 categories.
  - NOT_CLEARED cases still list their other failures, e.g.
    ACUTE_SAFETY_EXCLUSION + REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION.

  Distinct values:
  - Medications: the anticoagulants appear under both generic and brand names:
    - warfarin / Coumadin
    - apixaban / Eliquis
    - rivaroxaban / Xarelto
    - dabigatran / Pradaxa
    - edoxaban / Savaysa
  - Medication status:
    - 3 warfarin entries are active=False, and the Coumadin entry in case 33 has
      active=None.
    - The antiplatelets (ASA, aspirin, Aspirin EC, clopidogrel) need no plan.
  - Labs:
    - CBC appears as CBC and as LAB-CBC.
    - CMP appears as CMP and as LAB-CMP; BMP and LAB-BMP must not count as a
      CMP.
    - Status is final except for 3 entered-in-error CBCs.
  - Vitals: only blood_pressure and temperature (in value_f), with no null
    values. 5 cases have no temperature vital (cases 09, 11, 13, 21, 22), and
    case 22 has no vitals at all.
  - Documents: 39 distinct titles. There are 18 different H&P titles, including
    the typo "History and Pyhsical". There are 7 consent titles, including the
    "Pre-op Consent Discussion" in case 10, which is unsigned.

  Vitals in notes are always newest: in every one of the 50 cases, a note has a
  vital newer than the newest structured vital. So the "most recent vital" rule
  always depends on reading free text. Every NOT_CLEARED case with an elevated
  BP gets it from a note; the structured vitals look normal.

  Procedure fields:
  - procedure_risk is null in cases 29 and 39.
  - procedure_date is null in cases 11 and 44.
  - submission_received_at falls on the procedure date in all 48 cases that have
    a date.

  The difference from the other two categories: a missing or defective H&P or
  consent is still REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION (cases 08, 13, 40, 48), and a missing
  CBC or CMP is still REQUIRED_TESTING (cases 17, 26, 32, 36).
  MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA is only for an input the rule needs in order to be
  evaluated at all: the date, risk level, temperature or BP, or whether the
  patient is anticoagulated.

  What this means for the next step

  - Model choice: on this sample the problem isn't model capability. All three
    failures are in how the policy is interpreted, and deterministic rules would
    fix them.
  - Real risks: consistency between runs (no temperature setting, strict: False)
    and the fact that this is a single run on 50 cases. One wrong case moves
    the score by about 1.3–2 points. The next step should be make determinism
    MODEL=gpt-5.6-luna and a few repeated full runs to measure variance before
    changing anything.
  - Run archive: I saved this run's outputs and report to
    runs/00_baseline_asis_gpt-5.6-luna/. That folder isn't gitignored, and the
    make targets otherwise overwrite data/baseline_outputs.jsonl on every run.
  - Untouched files: the Python files are unchanged, core.py included. The only
    edits so far are the earlier Makefile .env loading and the one-line key fix
    in .env.

⏺ Now layer 2, the deterministic policy engine (policy_engine.py). It receives

⏺ The inconsistency comes from the audit trail, not the decisions: the
  deterministic guard always rejects procedure-risk statements like "Bleeding
  risk considered high," but that rejection only gets logged in
  audit.excluded_evidence on some runs. I'll now check if the other six varying
  cases share this same cause.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ I've traced the run-to-run variation to three causes: unneeded risk-statement
  extraction, a mangled document UUID (case 39, a real bug), and inconsistent
  quote spans (case 49). I'll fix each at the source—only request risk
  statements when the structured field is invalid, use short document IDs mapped
  back to real ones, and build the consent summary from deterministic values
  instead of model quotes.

  The scoring harness is unchanged: git diff shows run_evals.py, run_baseline.py
  and view_report.py identical to HEAD. In the Makefile, the existing recipes
  are untouched; I added .env loading, MODEL ?= gpt-5.6-luna and a new
  consistency target.

  Remaining variation (case 25): the decision is always READY. On some runs the
  model reports the aspirin/clopidogrel instructions as "anticoagulation plans".
  Python rejects them because they're antiplatelets, but that rejection is
  logged only on those runs, so the audit differs. The fix is small: skip known
  antiplatelets quietly instead of logging them. I haven't applied it, because
  that means re-running the full chain (about 10 minutes). Say if you want it.

  Why case 11 reports only MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA

  - Its only null field is procedure.procedure_date. The H&P says "date to be
    determined pending OR availability", so falling back to the documents finds
    nothing either.
  - There's no temperature anywhere. The structured vitals are BP only, the H&P
    records just NIBP 117/71, and the 03/09 note says "See flowsheet". "Negative
    for … fevers" is a symptom review, not a reading.
  - Both gaps fall under the same category. Without a date the H&P and CBC
    window checks can't run, so they're recorded as INDETERMINATE, not failed.

  The other four cases you asked about:
  - Cases 29 and 39: null risk, and the notes say "risk category not yet
    assigned".
  - Case 44: null date.
  - Case 33: Coumadin's active flag is null, and the note says "patient unable
    to confirm whether still taking".

  Design: two layers

  Layer 1 (extraction.py (preop-exercise/extraction.py))
  - Deterministic preprocessing. Lab codes are normalised; anything not final,
    invalid, or dated after the review time is excluded. Document titles are
    classified in Python, tolerating typos such as "History and Pyhsical". The
    medication list is classified against a drug-name list (anticoagulants and
    antiplatelets).
  - One structured extraction call per case. It uses a strict schema,
    temperature=0, reasoning: none and store=False. The model receives only
    documents (no patient identifiers, no policy) and returns facts with
    verbatim quotes.
  - Verification of every fact. Python checks that each quote exists in its
    document and that the value appears inside the quote. It re-derives
    temperature units from the text and checks plausibility. Facts that fail are
    rejected and logged in the audit.

  Layer 2 (policy_engine.py (preop-exercise/policy_engine.py)) is pure Python
  and makes every decision:
  - Dates and procedure details: all date arithmetic. Procedure date and risk
    come from the structured fields first; documents are used only when a field
    is null or invalid, and only an explicit statement in a document counts.
  - H&P and consent: a 30-day window for the H&P. Consent must be signed by the
    patient and name the scheduled procedure, matched strictly on wording.
  - Labs: CBC and CMP windows by risk level, and a BMP never counts as a CMP. If
    risk is unknown, a test is reported as failed only if it fails under every
    risk level.
  - Anticoagulants:
    - Only actual anticoagulants count.
    - active=false is ignored unless a current note confirms the patient is
      taking it.
    - Unknown status is reported as missing data.
    - A current anticoagulant needs both a hold and a resume instruction, each
      with a concrete time, and the plan must be for that same drug.
  - Vitals: structured readings are merged with readings from notes and ordered
    deterministically. Older abnormal readings never override newer ones;
    same-day readings that can't be ordered use the most severe one.

  core.py (preop-exercise/core.py) wires the two layers together. It reuses one
  OpenAI client (30s timeout, 2 retries) and raises an error rather than
  guessing when extraction fails. schemas.py (preop-exercise/schemas.py) and
  reference.py (preop-exercise/reference.py) hold the data contracts and the
  drug, lab and title reference data.

  Evidence and audit trail

  Each issue carries:
  - the rule ID and a plain-language summary;
  - the source IDs, using real document IDs;
  - quotes flagged verbatim, each snapped to the full source line.

  Each output also includes the policy and pipeline versions, how the procedure
  date and risk were resolved (source, raw value, notes), every rule's PASS /
  FAIL / INDETERMINATE / NOT_APPLICABLE result, excluded evidence with reasons,
  and warnings. A safety rule ensures any check that couldn't be evaluated
  prevents a READY decision.

  Right reasons, not just matching labels: 18 tricky cases match the human
  rationales on the specific values used. For example:
  - the "History and Pyhsical" H&P in case 01 is correctly counted (20 days);
  - the 30-day edge cases are correct: 30 days passes (case 12) and 31 days
    fails (case 14);
  - 148/110 is caught in case 28, and the 08:30 recheck of 137/78 supersedes
    189/112 in case 16;
  - 37.9 °C is correctly converted to 100.22 °F (case 30);
  - the most recent CBC being entered-in-error is handled in cases 04 and 06.

  Stability fixes made this round

  The model kept varying in ways that didn't change decisions, so I fixed each
  at the source; none hides model behaviour:
  1. Documents are given short IDs (D1, D2, …) because the model sometimes
     mangled the UUIDs. Python maps them back.
  2. Date and risk statements are requested from the documents only when the
     structured field is unusable.
  3. Evidence for the anticoagulation rule is every document line naming the
     drug, found by Python.
  4. Empty plan entries are dropped, and the summaries are built from
     deterministic values.

  Tests

  75 tests run fully offline (make test). They cover:
  - each rule and each boundary;
  - quotes and values the model invented, which must be rejected;
  - short-ID resolution and when the date/risk fallback is requested;
  - the API settings: strict schema, temperature=0, store=False, and the reused
    client with 30s timeout and 2 retries.

  run_consistency.py (preop-exercise/run_consistency.py) is new: it runs every
  case N times with fresh model calls (no cache).

  Choices you may want to revisit

  - Exact lab status: only final labs count, as you specified. Standard amended
    and corrected results would also be excluded; that's a one-line change to
    the accepted-status list in reference.py.
  - Lost cross-check: asking for date and risk statements only when needed means
    discrepancies between a valid structured field and the documents are no
    longer flagged.
  - Unordered same-day readings: structured readings are in UTC and note times
    are local wall-clock time, so a same-day mix is treated as unordered and the
    worst value is used.
  - Strict procedure matching: consent wording must match closely, so synonyms
    like "hip replacement" vs "arthroplasty" go to follow-up instead of passing.

  - Not done: no README design section yet, and no commits.
  - Untracked folder: runs/ holds the archived original-baseline outputs and
    isn't gitignored.

❯ once complete with that... let's also check how we're handling prompt caching
  and token telemetry in extraction.py. earlier you mentioned date and risk
  statements are only requested from documents when the structured field is
  unusable—if we change the system prompt or schema dynamically per case, won't
  that break openai's prefix ordering invariant and trigger a cache miss on
  those calls? let's make sure the schema and system instructions stay 100%
  static at the front of every request (and that the static prefix is at least
  1024 tokens, or uses a consistent prompt cache key for server routing) while
  any case-specific context goes at the very end of the user message, or
  policy_engine simply ignores extracted date/risk when the structured field is
  already present. also make sure we're capturing usage telemetry from the api
  response, such as the pomrpt tokens, cached tokens, and hit ratio so that we
  can verify the cache health across runs,. think production real world
  settings where the data might be messier and prep for such.

⏺ Summary of work since the first refactor

  Everything you asked for this round is done and verified offline (139 tests
  passing). The live check covered cases 16 and 25 only, as you asked: 3 runs
  each, byte-identical and matching their labels. I have not re-run the full 50
  cases since this round's changes. One make all && make consistency (about 10
  minutes) would confirm them.

  What changed, by request

  1. Pipeline rebuild (earlier rounds)
  - Layer 1, extraction.py:
    - Deterministic preprocessing of the structured fields.
    - One strict-schema extraction call per case (temperature=0, reasoning:
      none, store=False). The model receives documents only, with no patient
      identifiers.
    - Python checks every extracted fact against the source text: the quote must
      exist in the document, and the value must appear inside the quote.
  - Layer 2, policy_engine.py: pure Python. It does all the date arithmetic,
    ordering and unit conversion, applies the rules, and writes issues and the
    audit trail.
  - Harness: run_evals.py, run_baseline.py and view_report.py are unchanged from
    the repo.

  2. Run-to-run stability fixes (earlier rounds). Short document IDs (D1…)
  instead of UUIDs; date/risk fallback requested only when the structured field
  is unusable; evidence lines for anticoagulants found by Python; empty plan
  entries dropped. Byte-identical cases went from 42/50 to 49/50.

  3. This round
  - Case 25: known antiplatelets are now skipped before verification, for both
    plans and medication mentions. I found a second variant ("dual antiplatelet
    therapy" reported as an aspirin mention) and covered it too. Case 25 was
    then identical in 6 of 6 runs and again in 3 of 3.
  - Labs: amended and corrected are accepted alongside final.
  - Case 16: both BP readings are in the same 03/15 note, so the 08:30 recheck
    (137/78) correctly wins over 08:06 (189/112). The audit records the older
    reading as superseded.
  - UTC versus local times: for the general case you raised, there's a new
    PREOP_FACILITY_TIMEZONE setting (or a facility_timezone= argument). With it,
    UTC structured timestamps are converted to local time before ordering
    against note times. Without it, same-day readings that can't be ordered fall
    back to the most severe one, and the audit says why. You can't order them
    correctly without knowing the timezone.
  - NPO/fasting: a deterministic scan of nursing intake notes writes
    audit.informational_checks with one of five statuses. Tests confirm it never
    changes the decision or issues.
  - Prompt caching: the system prompt and schema (about 2,100 tokens, above the
    1,024 minimum) are identical on every request, and per-case data is the
    final user message. I added a versioned prompt_cache_key. Measured result:
    99.9% of input tokens cached on repeat runs, and about 2,100 shared-prefix
    tokens cached across different cases.
  - Telemetry: each call records input, cached, output and reasoning tokens, hit
    ratio, latency, response ID and model.
    - Missing usage fields are tolerated, and failed calls are reported too.
    - It is kept out of TriageOutput so outputs stay byte-identical, and is
      available through an on_telemetry hook and a log line.
    - make consistency now reports cache health overall and per run.
  - Comments: long generic docstrings were replaced with short comments on the
    tricky blocks, each saying how it connects to the other layer. An unused
    helper was removed.
  - Tests: 75 → 139, all offline. They include replays of recorded real
    extractions for cases 16 and 25, and new failure-mode tests:
    - Invented quotes: signature lines, plan quotes with altered timing,
      stitched "…" quotes, quotes attributed to the wrong document.
    - Mixed °C/°F strings (12 variants): °, ℃, "deg", "Celsius", text unit
      overriding the model's unit, unit inferred from range, exactly 38.0 °C.
    - Amended labs: superseding an older result, most-recent-only, dated after
      review time.
    - Brand versus generic: nine medication-list vs consult pairs, plus
      switching from one anticoagulant to another.
  - Unit detection hardened: "deg", "degrees", Celsius/Fahrenheit spelled out,
    and the Unicode ℃/℉ symbols.

  OpenAI cost

  Rates for gpt-5.6-luna from third-party price trackers (I couldn't confirm
  them on OpenAI's own page): $0.20 per 1M input tokens, $0.02 per 1M cached,
  $1.20 per 1M output. Measured per call: about 3,600–4,400 input tokens and
  650–950 output tokens.

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────┐
  │                  Scenario                   │ Approximate cost │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ One case, first call (shared prefix cached) │ ≈ $0.0012        │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ One case, exact repeat (about 99.9% cached) │ ≈ $0.0009        │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ One full 50-case baseline                   │ ≈ $0.06–0.08     │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ make all (baseline + 10 determinism runs)   │ ≈ $0.08–0.10     │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ make consistency (150 calls)                │ ≈ $0.15–0.20     │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ 10,000 cases per month                      │ ≈ $12–16         │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────┘

  About 70% of the cost is output tokens, so caching mostly reduces input cost
  and latency. A call takes about 4.3–4.8 seconds: a 50-case run is about 4
  minutes one at a time, or about 30 seconds with 8 in parallel.

  Edge-case coverage

  - Documentation: H&P title typos; exactly 30 days passes and 31 fails; H&P
    dated after the procedure; unsigned, pending or portal-only consents;
    consent for the wrong procedure; a newer consent replacing a pending one;
    procedure-name synonyms fail closed.
  - Labs: CBC/CMP code variants; BMP never counts as a CMP; code/display
    conflicts sent for review; any non-accepted status excluded; HIGH-risk
    14-day windows; unknown risk evaluated across all risk levels; timezone
    conversion that moves a lab across a window boundary.
  - Anticoagulants:
    - 16 anticoagulants and 10 antiplatelets, by generic and brand name.
    - active=false ignored unless a current note confirms use; unknown status
      reported as missing data.
    - Hold and resume instructions both required, each with a concrete time.
    - Plan must be for the same drug (handles switches between agents); agents
      prescribed elsewhere and found only in notes; unrecognised anticoagulants
      flagged for a pharmacist.
  - Vitals: readings from notes merged with structured readings; rechecks by
    time or by text order; °C/°F variants; implausible values rejected; readings
    after review time excluded; UTC versus local handling.
  - Procedure fields: missing or invalid date/risk fall back to the documents;
    possible field swaps are noted; structured values are never overridden by
    documents.
  - Data integrity:
    - Invented quotes, values or document IDs are rejected and logged.
    - Duplicate or undated documents are excluded.
    - A refusal or truncated response raises an error instead of guessing.
    - Any check that couldn't be evaluated prevents READY.

  Can this stay deterministic in production?

  - What is guaranteed: layer 2 is pure. The same verified facts always produce
    byte-identical output, and tests enforce this.
  - What isn't: the model's raw output varies even at temperature 0. The same
    input gave 676–742 output tokens across runs. Verification, line-snapping of
    quotes and evidence found by Python absorb that variation, which is how
    49–50/50 identical outputs were reached. Rare rejection-log differences can
    still occur on unusual charts.
  - Real-world risks:
    - gpt-5.6-luna has no dated snapshot in the model list, so OpenAI can update
      it silently. The prompt-version hash in the audit, the recorded fixtures
      and the consistency job are how you'd detect that.
    - Same-day UTC-versus-local ordering requires the facility timezone to be
      configured.
    - Dates are parsed as US M/D/YYYY, which would misread DD/MM charts.
    - Matching relies on English patterns.
    - Very large charts in a single call could hit the output limit; those fail
      closed rather than silently.
    - At high traffic, one cache key may need sharding.
  - Optional for production: cache extractions by document hash to make
    reprocessing idempotent. I deliberately didn't do that here, because it
    would hide nondeterminism from the eval.

  Files

  - Changed: core.py, extraction.py, policy_engine.py, schemas.py, reference.py,
    run_consistency.py, Makefile, .gitignore, tests/conftest.py,
    tests/test_policy_engine.py, tests/test_triage_submission.py.
  - New: tests/test_failure_modes.py, tests/test_recorded_cases.py,
    tests/fixtures/ (recorder script plus 2 recorded extractions).
  - Not committed.

  │ Label matches in   │ 48/50        │ 50/50             │ 50/50         │
  │ that run           │              │                   │               │
  └────────────────────┴──────────────┴───────────────────┴───────────────┘

  What the numbers mean:
  - Reasoning tokens: all 145 per case are eliminated (100%).
  - Output overall: up 225 tokens per case (+76%). The extraction JSON with
    quotes is bigger than the original's short decision, so moving reasoning to
    Python doesn't save output on balance.
  - Cost: about equal on a first call (1.05×) and about 27% cheaper on repeats
    (0.73×), entirely thanks to caching. The original's static prefix was under
    1,024 tokens, so it never cached.
  - Latency: about the same at p50 (+0.1–0.5 s). It's worse at p95 because of
    the longer outputs. Python's own work (preprocessing, verification, policy)
    adds 45 ms per case.

  Proof that caching works

  A controlled experiment with a brand-new prefix (a random nonce placed in
  front of the real prompt), using the real request shape:

  ┌───────────────────┬───────┬──────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
  │       Step        │ Input │  Cached  │              Shows               │
  ├───────────────────┼───────┼──────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ A: case 3, first  │ 4,343 │ 0        │ true cold start                  │
  │ call              │       │          │                                  │
  ├───────────────────┼───────┼──────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ B: case 3 again   │ 4,343 │ 4,340    │ the whole request caches         │
  │                   │       │ (99.9%)  │                                  │
  ├───────────────────┼───────┼──────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ C: case 7, same   │       │          │ a different case reuses exactly  │
  │ prefix            │ 3,867 │ 2,123    │ the static prefix, so case data  │
  │                   │       │          │ comes after it                   │
  ├───────────────────┼───────┼──────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ D: case 7, one    │       │          │ any change to the front breaks   │
  │ character changed │ 3,868 │ 0        │ caching, which is why            │
  │  at the front     │       │          │ instructions and schema never    │
  │                   │       │          │ vary per case                    │
  ├───────────────────┼───────┼──────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ E: empty payload  │ 2,153 │ 2,123    │ static prefix ≈ 2.1k tokens,     │
  │                   │       │          │ above the 1,024 minimum          │
  └───────────────────┴───────┴──────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

  Production calls agree. In the 150-call consistency run, 149 of 150 calls got
  a cache hit. The first-call pass averaged 2,058 cached tokens, about the size
  of the static prefix. Repeat passes had only 150 uncached tokens across 50
  calls.

  Where the next savings are

  Output dominates the cost. The model still reports antiplatelet mentions,
  which Python now discards, and it writes long quotes that Python snaps to
  whole lines anyway. Asking for anticoagulants only and shorter quotes would
  cut output. That's a prompt change, though: it means re-recording the fixtures
  and re-running the consistency check, so I've left it for you to decide.

  Housekeeping

  - run_consistency.py now also reports reasoning tokens, per-call averages and
    p95 latency.
  - Analysis scripts (original-request replay, cache experiment, TUI check) are
    in /tmp/preop_analysis/, outside the repo. I can move them into the repo if
    you want to keep them.
  - No commits made.

       69 -```bash

       79 +### 1. Two layers: extraction, then deterministic rules       
       80 +                                                              
       81  ```
       82 +submission ─► Layer 1  extraction.py ─────────────────────────
          +────► Layer 2  policy_engine.py ─► TriageOutput               
       83 +              preprocess structured fields (no model)         
          +      R0 procedure date/risk         decision                 
       84 +              one strict-schema model call over documents     
          +     R1 H&P window, signed consent  issues[] (+ evidence)     
       85 +              verify every fact against source text           
          +     R2 CBC / CMP windows           explanation               
       86 +                                                              
          +     R3 anticoagulation plan        audit                     
       87 +                                                              
          +     R4 BP / temperature exclusion                            
       88 +```                                                           
       89  
       73 -This opens a terminal UI (`view_report.py`) that shows per-cas
          -e results side-by-side with oracle expectations. You can brows
          -e records, see metric pass/fail status, and inspect submission
          - data. Press `f` on a metric row to filter the case list to fa
          -ilures. Press `q` to quit.                                    
       90 +**Layer 1 (`extraction.py`)**                                 
       91 +- **Structured fields, no model:**                            
       92 +  - Labs are normalised (`LAB-CBC` → CBC; a BMP never counts a
          +s a CMP).                                                     
       93 +  - Labs must be `final`, `amended` or `corrected`; anything d
          +ated after `submission_received_at` is excluded.              
       94 +  - Document titles are classified by type, tolerating typos (
          +"History and Pyhsical").                                      
       95 +  - Medication-list names are matched against 16 anticoagulant
          +s and 10 antiplatelets (generic and brand names) in `reference
          +.py`.                                                         
       96 +- **One model call per case** via `responses.parse` with a str
          +ict JSON schema, `temperature=0`, `reasoning.effort="none"`, `
          +store=False` and a versioned `prompt_cache_key`.              
       97 +  - The model receives documents only: no patient identifiers,
          + no policy, no structured fields.                             
       98 +  - It returns vitals, medication statements, hold/resume inst
          +ructions, consent signature status, H&P completion and, only w
          +hen the structured field is unusable, procedure date/risk stat
          +ements. Each item comes with a verbatim quote.                
       99 +- **Verification:** each fact must point to a real document, i
          +ts quote must exist in that document, and its value must appea
          +r inside the quote.                                           
      100 +  - Temperature units are taken from the text, not the model, 
          +and converted with `Decimal`.                                 
      101 +  - Documents are shown to the model as `D1`, `D2`, … because 
          +it was observed mangling UUIDs when copying them back.        
      102 +  - Facts that fail verification are logged in `audit.excluded
          +_evidence` and never reach Layer 2.                           
      103  
       75 -## Tests                                                      
      104 +**Layer 2 (`policy_engine.py`)** is pure Python: no model, clo
          +ck reads or randomness. It does all date arithmetic, recency o
          +rdering, unit thresholds and category mapping.                
      105 +- **Procedure fields:** structured `procedure_date`/`procedure
          +_risk` always win. Document statements are used only when the 
          +field is null or invalid, and only if they state the value exp
          +licitly.                                                      
      106 +- **Unknown inputs:**                                         
      107 +  - An unknown risk evaluates labs under every risk level, and
          + reports a failure only if the lab fails under all of them.   
      108 +  - An unknown date keeps the window checks INDETERMINATE inst
          +ead of failing them.                                          
      109 +  - An unknown anticoagulant status is `MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA`
          +, not `ANTICOAGULATION_MANAGEMENT`.                           
      110 +- **Anticoagulation plans:**                                  
      111 +  - A plan needs both a hold and a resume instruction, each wi
          +th a concrete time.                                           
      112 +  - The plan must be for the drug the patient is taking now, m
          +atched across brand and generic names.                        
      113 +  - Antiplatelets never require a plan.                       
      114 +- **Vitals:** a later recheck always supersedes an earlier abn
          +ormal reading. Same-day readings that can't be ordered (UTC st
          +ructured timestamps vs local note times with no facility timez
          +one) use the most severe value, and the audit says why.       
      115 +- **Safety rule:** a rule that couldn't be evaluated can never
          + produce READY.

      145 +                                                              
      146 +### 3. Audit trail: how a reviewer verifies a decision        
      147 +                                                              
      148 +Every output carries `audit`, which contains:                 
      149 +- the policy and prompt versions;                             
      150 +- how procedure date and risk were resolved (source, raw value
          +, notes);                                                     
      151 +- a `rule_checks` entry for every rule: `PASS` / `FAIL` / `IND
          +ETERMINATE` / `NOT_APPLICABLE`, each with a summary, source ID
          +s and quotes;                                                 
      152 +- `excluded_evidence`, listing data that was ignored and why; 
      153 +- `warnings` and `informational_checks`.                      
      154 +                                                              
      155 +Quotes marked `verbatim: true` are whole lines copied from the
          + source document, so a nurse can search the chart for them dir
          +ectly. Quotes marked `verbatim: false` are renderings of struc
          +tured rows (lab, vital, medication-list entry). Issues reuse t
          +he evidence of the rule check that failed.                    
      156 +                                                              
      157 +- **Blocked case (case 05, NOT_CLEARED):** `R4.BLOOD_PRESSURE`
          + reports "most recent blood pressure BP 157/117 mmHg at 2026-0
          +3-03 09:05 … meets SBP >= 180 or DBP >= 110". It quotes the nu
          +rsing note line `09:05  BP 157/117, Temp 36.7 °C, HR 69, SpO2 
          +99% RA`. The structured vitals looked normal, and the audit sh
          +ows exactly which newer reading drove the block.              
      158 +- **READY case worth sampling (case 16):** `R4.BLOOD_PRESSURE`
          + passes on the `08:30` recheck (137/78) and lists the supersed
          +ed `08:06` reading of 189/112 with both source lines. `R1.SURG
          +ICAL_CONSENT` quotes the patient signature line and the proced
          +ure line. A reviewer sampling READY cases can check every rule
          + against its quoted line without opening the full chart.      
      159 +- **Follow-up case (case 18):** `R3.ANTICOAGULATION.APIXABAN` 
          +fails with the medication-list entry and the note line "warfar
          +in was stopped last month and replaced with Eliquis". Warfarin
          + is `NOT_APPLICABLE`, and the evidence shows that its plan pre
          +dates the switch.                                             
      160 +                                                              
      161 +### 4. Clinical workflow boundary                             
      162 +                                                              
      163 +This came out of discussions with the owner of an electrophysi
          +ology clinic. Items such as NPO/fasting instructions are about
          + adherence on the day of surgery: nursing staff confirm them a
          +t intake and on the day itself. They aren't criteria for wheth
          +er a case can be scheduled. So the scheduling gate covers only
          + the appendix policy (Rules 1–4); the pipeline adds no criteri
          +a of its own.                                                 
      164 +                                                              
      165 +NPO review is still useful to the nurse, so a deterministic sc
          +an of nursing intake notes records it in `audit.informational_
          +checks` with one of five statuses:                            
      166 +- `REVIEWED_WITH_PATIENT`                                     
      167 +- `INSTRUCTIONS_DOCUMENTED`                                   
      168 +- `NOT_REVIEWED`                                              
      169 +- `NOT_DOCUMENTED`                                            
      170 +- `NO_NURSING_INTAKE_NOTE`                                    
      171 +                                                              
      172 +These checks are marked `affects_decision: false`, live outsid
          +e `rule_checks`, and are covered by tests showing they never c
          +hange the decision or issues.                                 
      173 +                                                              
      174 +## Code map                                                   
      175 +                                                              
      176 +| File | Role |                                               
      177 +|---|---|                                                     
      178 +| `core.py` | entry point: shared OpenAI client (30 s timeout,
          + 2 retries), facility timezone, telemetry hook |              
      179 +| `extraction.py` | Layer 1: preprocessing, prompt and strict 
          +schema, model call, usage telemetry, verification |           
      180 +| `policy_engine.py` | Layer 2: rules, issues, explanation, au
          +dit trail, informational checks |                             
      181 +| `reference.py` | drug, lab and title reference data; date/ti
          +me, quote and procedure-matching helpers |                    
      182 +| `schemas.py` | input, output and audit models |             
      183 +| `run_consistency.py` | multi-run consistency and telemetry r
          +eport |                                                       
      184 +| `tests/` | offline tests; `tests/fixtures/` holds recorded r
          +eal extractions plus the script that records them |           
      185 +                                                              
      186 +## Known limits                                               
      187 +                                                              
      188 +- **Timezone:** same-day ordering of UTC structured vitals aga
          +inst note times needs `PREOP_FACILITY_TIMEZONE`. Without it th
          +e most severe reading is used.                                
      189 +- **Procedure names:** consent matching is strict word matchin
          +g, so synonyms ("hip replacement" vs "arthroplasty") go to fol
          +low-up instead of passing.                                    
      190 +- **Locale:** dates are parsed as US `M/D/YYYY`, and the text 
          +patterns are English.                                         
      191 +- **Large charts:** one model call per case. A chart large eno
          +ugh to hit the output limit raises `ExtractionError` for manua
          +l review; there's no chunking.                                
      192 +- **Model drift:** `gpt-5.6-luna` has no dated snapshot. The p
          +rompt-version hash in every audit, the recorded fixtures and `
          +make consistency` are how drift would be detected.            
      193 +- **Runtime failures:** model refusals or truncated responses 
          +raise `ExtractionError` instead of producing a guessed decisio
          +n.

⏺ It's a good experiment, and it doesn't need to touch the frozen code. The only
  incompatibility is that gpt-4.1-mini rejects the reasoning parameter, which
  the pipeline always sends. So the separate implementation is a small script in
  /tmp/preop_analysis/, outside the repo. It hands the unchanged
  triage_submission a thin client wrapper that drops reasoning and passes
  everything else through untouched: same prompt, strict schema, temperature=0,
  store=False and prompt_cache_key.

  The script will:
  - run all 50 cases 3 times (8 workers) with telemetry;
  - replay case 0 ten times, matching what make determinism measures;
  - write the outputs in the harness format, so the unmodified run_evals.py can
    score them.

⏺ Found the root cause. gpt-4.1-mini corrupts the degree sign when it writes
  JSON: every ° in a quote came back as a control character followed by "6"
  (36.7 \x176C instead of 36.7 °C). Every quoted vitals line with a degree sign
  failed verbatim matching, so the abnormal readings in cases 2, 5 and 47 were
  dropped. gpt-5.6-luna doesn't have this problem.

  Your two-layer pipeline on gpt-4.1-mini

  This was a separate experiment: the frozen repo code, run through a client
  wrapper that drops only the reasoning parameter. Everything else is unchanged
  (temperature=0, strict schema, prompt_cache_key, store=False), and the outputs
  were scored with the unmodified run_evals.py.

  What this shows:
  - On the same model, the architecture is what fixes accuracy: 74.67% → 96.0%.
    The pipeline's decisions were also stable across runs, which the starter's
    weren't (the starter got case 0 consistently wrong).
  - On gpt-4.1-mini it costs more, not less. Output grows 2.6× at $1.60 per 1M
    output tokens, and caching is weaker, so it's 31% more expensive on first
    calls and about equal on repeats. The cost savings in your write-up come
    from pairing the architecture with gpt-5.6-luna.
  - Determinism depends on the model too. At temperature=0, gpt-4.1-mini
    produced byte-identical output in only 32 of 50 cases.

  A safety gap this exposed (it applies to any model, not just gpt-4.1-mini):
  verification correctly rejects quotes that aren't verbatim, but a rejected
  real abnormal reading is then simply ignored. The decision falls back to
  older, normal readings, which is unsafe for Rule 4. The fix would be to treat
  a rejected vital that's newer than any accepted reading as
  MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA (send to review) rather than dropping it. That changes
  frozen code, so I haven't touched it. It's a few lines plus tests, and it
  doesn't affect the gpt-5.6-luna results, since no vitals were rejected there.

  The experiment lives entirely in /tmp/preop_analysis/: the script
  pipeline_gpt41mini.py, plus the outputs, eval report and
  experiment_report.json in pipeline_gpt-4.1-mini/. The repo is unchanged. I can
  add it as a fourth folder in results/ and a README row, or apply the safety
  fix, if you want either.

⏺ I've confirmed reference.normalize_for_match normalizes degree-sign variants
  (corrupted control chars, escaped \u00b0, look-alikes like º/˚/∘) to °, treats
  it as optional, and strips stray control characters—while leaving evidence
  text as the original source line and only affecting matching logic.

      353  def normalize_for_match(value: str) -> str:
      354 +    # Used only to *compare* model quotes with source text; ev
          +idence keeps the source line.                                 
      355      value = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", value)

  runs, breaking the "36.7 ºC" case in both extraction.py checks. I'll add a
  shared fold_degree_signs helper that runs before (and once after) NFKC to fix
  this.

⏺ I found three logic bugs: the H&P and labs checks don't filter to
  pre-procedure results, and the consent check fails valid forms when the model
  omits the procedure name instead of falling back to the Procedure: line. None
  affect the current frozen outputs, but I'll fix all three and add an audit
  warning for cases where the procedure date precedes the review time.

⏺ Written for: the Cadence reviewers. The README is for anyone running the repo;
  writeup_draft.md is your draft to edit in Google Docs.

  Everything is done: 159 offline tests pass, the starter harness files are
  unchanged, and nothing is committed or pushed. The prompt, schema and frozen
  gpt-5.6-luna outputs are untouched, and no live API calls were needed.

  Fail-closed vitals (pipeline 2.1.0):
  - The rule: a vital that fails verification and isn't superseded by a newer
    verified reading of the same type now makes Rule 4 INDETERMINATE and adds
    MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA, so the chart goes to nurse review. An unverified
    value is never used to issue NOT_CLEARED either.
  - Structured vitals too: malformed ones (missing date or values, implausible,
    fractional BP) are handled the same way. Previously 179.6 was silently
    truncated to 179.
  - Frozen outputs unaffected: I checked all 50 frozen gpt-5.6-luna outputs;
    none had a rejected vital.
  - Degree signs: quote matching now folds the gpt-4.1-mini corruption plus º,
    ˚, ℃ and escaped \u00b0. This exposed a second bug in my first attempt,
    where Unicode normalisation turned º into the letter "o".

  Rule review: I checked every rule against the policy text and fixed three
  latent bugs the sample data never triggered:
  - an H&P dated after the procedure could hide a valid earlier one;
  - a lab dated after the procedure could do the same;
  - a signed consent failed if the model omitted the procedure name (Python now
    reads the form's Procedure: line).

  There's also a new warning when the procedure date is earlier than the review
  time. Several tests had encoded the old silent-fallback behaviour; I updated
  them, and one turned out to have a stale fixture that only passed because of
  that fallback.

  Hypothesis:
  - Setup: uv add --dev needs a pyproject.toml and the repo had none, so I
    created a minimal one with uv init --bare. Runtime deps are openai and
    pydantic; dev deps are pytest and hypothesis; uv.lock pins versions.
  - Tests: tests/test_properties.py holds 10 properties. A stress run of 30,000
    examples found no failures.
  - Mutation check: it showed the threshold tests could miss a 180 → 181 change,
    so they now oversample boundary values. After that, all 8 deliberate bugs
    are caught.

  Docs and results:
  - results/: now covers four configurations, including the gpt-4.1-mini
    pipeline experiment, with a provenance README. Scratch work stays in /tmp.
  - Top-level README: build/test changes from the starter, the four-way tables,
    a testing section, and the limits.

  Where the write-up differs from your framing (please check these):
  - Byte-identical outputs come from Layer 2 (line-snapped quotes, evidence
    found by Python, a pure-Python policy). temperature=0 and reasoning: none
    reduce variation, and caching reduces cost; neither creates determinism on
    its own.
  - Output tokens: about 515 against 151–297 for the starter is not "slightly
    more". The draft says so and shows why it's worth it.
  - Starter errors on gpt-4.1-mini: I replaced an example I couldn't verify with
    the measured error breakdown.
  - Nurse review is described as how the system is built for production, not as
    something that already happened.
  - The electrophysiology clinic note reflects only what you told me.

❯ wait we didnt remeasure the gpt 4.1 -mini with the new fixes?? also we shoudl

⏺ You're right: the gpt-4.1-mini numbers are from 2.0.0, and the README says so
  ("not re-measured live"). Re-measuring is a real API run of about 160 calls
  (roughly $0.30), so I'll do it now that you've asked.

  On synonyms, here is the risk as it stands. Consent matching is strict word
  matching, so "hip replacement" vs "total hip arthroplasty" fails closed into
  follow-up. Reviewing that code also turned up a real wrong-site bug: left,
  right and bilateral are treated as stopwords, so a consent for a "right knee
  arthroscopy" would match a scheduled "left knee arthroscopy".

  So I'll make the procedure-matching changes first and then re-measure, so the
  live numbers reflect the final code:
  1. Laterality conflicts always mismatch.
  2. Deterministic synonyms: a small, reviewable table of synonyms and
     abbreviations (CABG, TKA/THA, replacement ↔ arthroplasty, herniorrhaphy,
     …).
  3. An optional AI matcher, off by default. It runs only when strict matching
     fails, and it can only suggest a match for the nurse to confirm; it never
     passes a consent on its own.

      124    - A plan needs both a hold and a resume instruction, each wi
           th a concrete time.
      125    - The plan must be for the drug the patient is taking now, m

      126    - Antiplatelets never require a plan.
      127 +- **Consent:** must be signed by the patient and name the sche
          +duled procedure. Names are compared after a reviewed synonym/a
          +bbreviation table (`reference.PROCEDURE_SYNONYMS`), and a left
          +/right conflict always fails (wrong-site). Anything else that 
          +doesn't match fails closed into `REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION`. If t
          +he optional `procedure_matcher` (`ai_procedure_matcher.py`) is
          + passed to `triage_submission`, a separate small model call ad
          +ds an `INFO.PROCEDURE_EQUIVALENCE` suggestion for the nurse; i
          +t never passes the consent. Confirmed pairs go into the synony
          +m table.

           e count. "Most recent result" means the most recent pre-proced
           ure result.
      129  - **Vitals:** a later recheck always supersedes an earlier abn
           ormal reading. Same-day readings that can't be ordered (UTC st
           ructured timestamps vs local note times with no facility timez
           one) use the most severe value, and the audit says why.
      126 -- **Fail-closed vitals:** suppose a reading failed verificatio
          -n (quote not in the note, value not in the quote, implausible 
          -or malformed) and no newer verified reading of the same type s
          -upersedes it. Then Rule 4 is INDETERMINATE and the case gets `
          -MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA`, so a nurse confirms the latest value. 
          -Before this rule, an unverifiable 157/117 silently fell back t
          -o an older normal reading and returned READY. An unverified va
          -lue is never used to issue NOT_CLEARED either.

  ⎿  Created WRITEUP.md (+155 -0)
        1 +# Pre-op triage: approach and design decisions                
        2 +                                                              
        3 +## Problem                                                    
        4 +                                                              
        5 +A surgery can't be scheduled until documentation, testing and 
          +risk checks are complete. For each patient package the system 
          +decides whether the case can be scheduled (READY / NEEDS_FOLLO
          +W_UP / NOT_CLEARED) and lists every missing or blocking item, 
          +with evidence. The policy has four rules: H&P and signed conse
          +nt; CBC/CMP windows by procedure risk; an anticoagulation plan
          +; acute BP and temperature exclusions. Any data needed to eval
          +uate a rule that is missing means follow-up.                  
        6 +                                                              
        7 +The starter code asked one model call to read the chart and ap
          +ply the policy. On the repo's default model (`gpt-4.1-mini`) i
          +t scored 74.67%, and most of its errors were in applying the p
          +olicy:                                                        
        8 +- it flagged 9 READY cases as NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP;                
        9 +- it raised 7 spurious anticoagulation issues and 6 spurious t
          +esting issues;                                                
       10 +- it missed 6 `MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA` cases;                  
       11 +- it got acute-safety exclusions wrong in both directions (2 m
          +issed, 3 spurious).                                           
       12 +                                                              
       13 +Even on `gpt-5.6-luna` (97.33%), the remaining misses were pol
          +icy errors: aspirin treated as an anticoagulant, window violat
          +ions reported when there was no procedure date, and an unknown
          + anticoagulant status counted twice.                          
       14 +                                                              
       15 +## 1. Architecture: two layers                                
       16 +                                                              
       17 +**Layer 1: extraction (the model reads, Python checks).**     
       18 +- **Structured fields, normalised without a model:**          
       19 +  - lab codes (`LAB-CBC` → CBC; a BMP is never a CMP);        
       20 +  - lab statuses (`final`, `amended` and `corrected` count);  
       21 +  - document titles, tolerating typos ("History and Pyhsical")
          +;                                                             
       22 +  - medication names against 16 anticoagulants and 10 antiplat
          +elets, by generic and brand name.                             
       23 +- **One model call per case**, using a strict JSON schema, `te
          +mperature=0`, `reasoning.effort="none"`, `store=False` and a v
          +ersioned prompt cache key.                                    
       24 +  - The model sees documents only: no patient identifiers, no 
          +policy, no structured fields.                                 
       25 +  - It returns facts (vitals, medication statements, hold/resu
          +me instructions, consent signature status, H&P completion), ea
          +ch with a verbatim quote.                                     
       26 +- **Python verifies every fact:**                             
       27 +  - the document exists;                                      
       28 +  - the quote is in that document;                            
       29 +  - the value is inside the quote;                            
       30 +  - the temperature unit is the one written next to the number
          +.                                                             
       31 +                                                              
       32 +  Anything that fails is logged and never used as a fact.     
       33 +                                                              
       34 +**Layer 2: a deterministic policy engine (Python only).**     
       35 +- It handles all date arithmetic, ordering of readings, unit c
          +onversion (`Decimal`, so 38.0 °C is exactly 100.4 °F) and cate
          +gory mapping.                                                 
       36 +- Every rule writes a PASS / FAIL / INDETERMINATE / NOT_APPLIC
          +ABLE check, with a summary, source IDs and quotes. Each FAIL b
          +ecomes an issue.                                              
       37 +- Structured procedure fields always win; documents are only a
          + fallback for null or invalid values.                         
       38 +- Unknown inputs become `MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA` rather than gu
          +esses. For example, an unknown risk level means labs are evalu
          +ated under every level, and a failure is reported only if the 
          +lab fails under all of them.                                  
       39 +- A rule that couldn't be evaluated can never produce READY.  
       40 +                                                              
     … 115 more lines (ctrl+o to expand)

  The three cases it used to miss (2, 5 and 47) are now correctly NOT_CLEARED,

  run is mentioned once in the README and write-up only as the story of how the
  bug was found.

  Synonym handling and the bug it uncovered:
  - Wrong-site bug fixed: "left", "right" and "bilateral" were ignored when
    matching consent procedures, so a right-knee consent matched a left-knee
    schedule. A left/right conflict now always fails as a wrong-site consent.
  - Synonym table: a small, reviewable list of synonyms and abbreviations (CABG,
    TKA/THA, replacement ↔ arthroplasty, herniorrhaphy, RYGB, …).
  - AI matcher: ai_procedure_matcher.py is off by default and only runs when a
    consent's procedure name fails the deterministic match. It adds a suggestion
    for the nurse and never passes a consent on its own; confirmed pairs go
    into the table. It's a separate request, so the main extraction prompt and
    its caching are unchanged.

  I checked all 49 consent procedure lines in the dataset: none changes outcome
  under the new matching.

  aren't broken: every later change was checked against them and alters no

  keeping all date math, recency sorting, pharmacology classification, and
  procedure matching in python eliminates model improvisation, while pairing
  temperature=0 and reasoning="none" with a 2,100-token static prefix and
  prompt_cache_key lets us trade a modest increase in output tokens (for
  verifiable quotes) in exchange for a high cache hit rate and 50/50
  byte-identical outputs once python snaps quotes to source lines. in the
  second paragraph, walk through why running the exact same pipeline on
  gpt-4.1-mini was so important for proving that the architecture itself drives
  the accuracy jump from 74.67% to 96.0%, and how observing degree-symbol
  serialization issues on that smaller model motivated our fail-closed rule
  where any unverified or malformed latest vital sign routes the chart to nurse
  review (missing_required_data) rather than falling back to older normal
  readings, locked down by our offline pytest and hypothesis property suite. in
  the final paragraph, explain the reasoning behind the evidence packet and
  audit ledger—showing how surfacing both blocking provenance and excluded
  records helps a pre-op nurse verify blocked charts and sampled ready charts
  in seconds, and how i went an had a conversation with an electrophysiology
  clinic owner about common day-of-surgery patient adherence failures (like
  forgetting fasting/npo instructions or hold windows) led me to track npo
  review status and ensured the compelte focus on having strong audit trail.

        1 -# Pre-Op Triage Take-Home                                     
        1 +# Pre-Op Triage                                               
        2  
        3 -## Objective                                                  
        3 +Decides whether a surgical case can be scheduled (READY / NEED
          +S_FOLLOW_UP / NOT_CLEARED) and lists every missing or blocking
          + item with its evidence.                                      
        4  
        5 -Implement `triage_submission(...)` in `core.py` - it is a pre-
          -op triage function for a single submission package. It is curr
          -ently a naive LLM-based solution that makes a real model API c
          -all. The starter implementation intentionally does not follow 
          -some best practices in using the OpenAI API. You may use whate
          -ver file structure makes sense for your solution.             
        5 +## Run                                                        
        6  
        7 -Your output must match this schema:                           
        8 -                                                              
        9 -- `decision`: `READY | NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP | NOT_CLEARED`         
       10 -- `issues[]`: `category` + evidence (how you design this is up
          - to you)                                                      
       11 -- `explanation`                                               
       12 -                                                              
       13 -## What Is Provided                                           
       14 -                                                              
       15 -- `data/patients_sample_50.jsonl` includes:                   
       16 -  - `case_id`                                                 
       17 -  - `submission`                                              
       18 -  - `label` (what a human labeled this case as)               
       19 -- `run_baseline.py` runs your `triage_submission` implementati
          -on and writes outputs.                                        
       20 -- `run_evals.py` scores outputs against provided `label` and c
          -an run determinism checks.                                    
       21 -                                                              
       22 -## Completion                                                 
       23 -                                                              
       24 -Note: this exercise is evaluated on engineering judgment. You 
          -may not reach a 100% score, and that is OK! We are looking to 
          -understand how you approached the problem and designed a worki
          -ng solution.

       27 -                                                              
       28 -# Solution                                                    
       29 -                                                              
       30 -The model reads the documents and returns facts with verbatim 
          -quotes. Python checks every quote against the source text, the
          -n applies the policy deterministically. The provided harness (
          -`run_baseline.py`, `run_evals.py`, `view_report.py`) is unchan
          -ged.                                                          
       31 -                                                              
       32 -The full write-up of the approach and design decisions is in [
          -WRITEUP.md](WRITEUP.md). This README covers running the code, 
          -results, design and limits.                                   
       33 -

       44 -### What changed from the starter setup                       
       45 -- **Dependencies:** `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` declare the
          -m: `openai` and `pydantic` at runtime; `pytest` and `hypothesi
          -s` in the dev group. `make test` now runs `uv run python -m py
          -test tests` in that environment instead of `uv run --with ...`
          -.                                                             
       46 -- **Makefile:** loads `.env`, defaults `MODEL` to `gpt-5.6-lun
          -a`, and adds `make consistency`.                              
       47 -- **Unchanged:** `make baseline`, `make evals`, `make determin

       18 +## Approach and design decisions                              
       19  
       49 -## Commands                                                   
       20 +I split the system into a constrained extraction layer and a p
          +ure Python policy engine so the model does only what it is goo
          +d at. One strict-schema call reads the chart and returns facts
          + (vitals, medication statements, hold and resume instructions,
          + consent signatures), each with a verbatim quote. Everything t
          +hat must be exact lives in Python: date math, recency sorting,
          + unit conversion, anticoagulant versus antiplatelet classifica
          +tion, and procedure matching. The model never sees the policy,
          + so it has nothing to improvise, and Python rejects any quote 
          +it cannot find in the source. Once the policy moved into code,
          + reasoning tokens were pure cost, so I pair `temperature=0` wi
          +th `reasoning="none"`. Instructions and schema form a byte-ide
          +ntical 2,100-token prefix, above the 1,024-token caching thres
          +hold and routed with a versioned `prompt_cache_key`, with ever
          +y case-specific detail after it. Verbatim quotes cost more out
          +put tokens than a bare decision, but in exchange I get a 54% c
          +ache hit on first calls and 99.9% on repeats. And because Pyth
          +on snaps every quote to its full source line, the outputs were
          + byte-identical on all 50 cases across repeated runs.         
       21  
       51 -| Command | What it does | Needs API key |                    
       52 -|---|---|---|                                                 
       53 -| `make test` | 173 offline tests, including Hypothesis proper
          -ty tests (`HYPOTHESIS_PROFILE=stress make test` runs 3,000 exa
          -mples per property) | no |                                    
       54 -| `make baseline` | runs all 50 cases → `data/baseline_outputs
          -.jsonl` | yes |                                               
       55 -| `make evals` | scores with the provided harness → `data/eval
          -_report.json` | yes |                                         
       56 -| `make determinism` | provided 10× replay of one record → `da
          -ta/determinism_report.json` | yes |

       59 -| `make consistency` | every case `RUNS` times (default 3), fr
          -esh model calls, 8 workers → `data/consistency_report.json` wi
          -th byte-identity, label match and token/cache/latency telemetr
          -y | yes |                                                     
       60 -| `make report` | interactive TUI over the eval report | no | 
       22 +To prove the architecture, not the model, drives accuracy, I r
          +an the same pipeline on gpt-4.1-mini, the repository's default
          +. The starter code scores 74.67% there; the pipeline scored 96
          +.0% on its first run. The misses mattered more than the score.
          + The smaller model corrupted the degree symbol when serializin
          +g quotes, so Python correctly rejected genuinely abnormal vita
          +ls, but Rule 4 then fell back to older normal readings and cle
          +ared those charts. I made the rule fail closed: if the latest 
          +vital is unverified or malformed and no newer verified reading
          + supersedes it, the chart goes to nurse review as `MISSING_REQ
          +UIRED_DATA`, and an unverified value can never trigger an excl
          +usion. With that rule and degree-sign normalization, gpt-4.1-m
          +ini reached 100%. An offline pytest suite with Hypothesis prop
          +erties pins these invariants across arbitrary dates, and a mut
          +ation check confirmed it catches off-by-one thresholds and fai
          +l-open regressions.                                           
       23  
       62 -Configuration:                                                
       24 +The output is built for the pre-op nurse who has to trust it. 
          +Each issue carries the failed rule, a plain summary, source ID
          +s and verbatim-flagged quotes. The audit ledger records every 
          +rule that passed, how procedure date and risk were resolved, a
          +nd every excluded record with its reason, so a nurse can confi
          +rm a blocked chart or spot-check a ready one in seconds. I als
          +o spoke with the owner of an electrophysiology clinic about wh
          +ere cases fall apart, and the answer was day-of-surgery adhere
          +nce: patients forgetting fasting instructions or anticoagulant
          + hold windows. Hold windows are already in the policy. NPO rev
          +iew is not, so I track it as an informational nurse check in t
          +he audit trail, with complete provenance, while the scheduling
          + gate stays strictly bound to the appendix policy.

       86 -The starter code fails for the same reason on both models: the
          - model applies the policy itself. On `gpt-5.6-luna` it treated
          - aspirin as an anticoagulant, reported window violations when 
          -there was no procedure date, and double-counted an unknown ant
          -icoagulant status. On `gpt-4.1-mini` it misses 29 of 50 cases.
          - The same two-layer pipeline on `gpt-4.1-mini` gets all 50, so
          - the architecture, not the model, does the work.

       88 -On the way there, the first `gpt-4.1-mini` pipeline run (2.0.0
          -) missed 3 acute-safety cases. The model corrupted the degree 
          -sign in its quotes, Python correctly rejected them, and Rule 4
          - then fell back to older normal vitals. That led to the degree
          --sign normalisation and the fail-closed vitals rule described 
          -below. Re-measured on the current code, all three are NOT_CLEA
