       89 -                                                              
       90 -## Design                                                     
       91 -                                                              
       92 -### 1. Two layers: extraction, then deterministic rules       
       93 -                                                              
       94 -```                                                           
       95 -submission ─► Layer 1  extraction.py ─────────────────────────
          -────► Layer 2  policy_engine.py ─► TriageOutput               
       96 -              preprocess structured fields (no model)         
          -      R0 procedure date/risk         decision                 
       97 -              one strict-schema model call over documents     
          -     R1 H&P window, signed consent  issues[] (+ evidence)     
       98 -              verify every fact against source text           
          -     R2 CBC / CMP windows           explanation               
       99 -                                                              
          -     R3 anticoagulation plan        audit                     
      100 -                                                              
          -     R4 BP / temperature exclusion                            
      101 -```                                                           
      102 -                                                              
      103 -**Layer 1 (`extraction.py`)**                                 
      104 -- **Structured fields, no model:**                            
      105 -  - Labs are normalised (`LAB-CBC` → CBC; a BMP never counts a
          -s a CMP).                                                     
      106 -  - Labs must be `final`, `amended` or `corrected`; anything d
          -ated after `submission_received_at` is excluded.              
      107 -  - Document titles are classified by type, tolerating typos (
          -"History and Pyhsical").                                      
      108 -  - Medication-list names are matched against 16 anticoagulant
          -s and 10 antiplatelets (generic and brand names) in `reference
          -.py`.                                                         
      109 -- **One model call per case** via `responses.parse` with a str
          -ict JSON schema, `temperature=0`, `reasoning.effort="none"`, `
          -store=False` and a versioned `prompt_cache_key`.              
      110 -  - The model receives documents only: no patient identifiers,
          - no policy, no structured fields.                             
      111 -  - It returns vitals, medication statements, hold/resume inst
          -ructions, consent signature status, H&P completion and, only w
          -hen the structured field is unusable, procedure date/risk stat
          -ements. Each item comes with a verbatim quote.                
      112 -- **Verification:** each fact must point to a real document, i
          -ts quote must exist in that document, and its value must appea
          -r inside the quote.                                           
      113 -  - Temperature units are taken from the text, not the model, 
          -and converted with `Decimal`.                                 
      114 -  - Documents are shown to the model as `D1`, `D2`, … because 
          -it was observed mangling UUIDs when copying them back.        
      115 -  - Facts that fail verification are logged in `audit.excluded
          -_evidence` and are never used as facts.                       
      116 -  - Vitals that fail verification are also kept as *unverified
          -* readings, so that Rule 4 can fail closed (below).           
      117 -  - Before comparison, quote matching folds degree-sign varian
          -ts: `gpt-4.1-mini` emitted a control character plus "6" in pla
          -ce of "°", and `º`, `˚` and `℃` also appear in real text. Evid
          -ence still shows the original source line.                    
      118 -                                                              
      119 -**Layer 2 (`policy_engine.py`)** is pure Python: no model, clo
          -ck reads or randomness. It does all date arithmetic, recency o
          -rdering, unit thresholds and category mapping.                
      120 -- **Procedure fields:** structured `procedure_date`/`procedure
          -_risk` always win. Document statements are used only when the 
          -field is null or invalid, and only if they state the value exp
          -licitly.                                                      
      121 -- **Unknown inputs:**                                         
      122 -  - An unknown risk evaluates labs under every risk level, and
          - reports a failure only if the lab fails under all of them.   
      123 -  - An unknown date keeps the window checks INDETERMINATE inst
          -ead of failing them.                                          
      124 -  - An unknown anticoagulant status is `MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA`
          -, not `ANTICOAGULATION_MANAGEMENT`.                           
      125 -- **Anticoagulation plans:**                                  
      126 -  - A plan needs both a hold and a resume instruction, each wi
          -th a concrete time.                                           
      127 -  - The plan must be for the drug the patient is taking now, m
          -atched across brand and generic names.                        
      128 -  - Antiplatelets never require a plan.                       
      129 -- **Consent:** must be signed by the patient and name the sche
          -duled procedure. Names are compared after a reviewed synonym/a
          -bbreviation table (`reference.PROCEDURE_SYNONYMS`), and a left
          -/right conflict always fails (wrong-site). Anything else that 
          -doesn't match fails closed into `REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION`. If t
          -he optional `procedure_matcher` (`ai_procedure_matcher.py`) is
          - passed to `triage_submission`, a separate small model call ad
          -ds an `INFO.PROCEDURE_EQUIVALENCE` suggestion for the nurse; i
          -t never passes the consent. Confirmed pairs go into the synony
          -m table.                                                      
      130 -- **H&P and labs:** only items dated on or before the procedur
          -e count. "Most recent result" means the most recent pre-proced
          -ure result.                                                   
      131 -- **Vitals:** a later recheck always supersedes an earlier abn
          -ormal reading. Same-day readings that can't be ordered (UTC st
          -ructured timestamps vs local note times with no facility timez
          -one) use the most severe value, and the audit says why.       
      132 -- **Fail-closed vitals:** suppose a reading failed verificatio
          -n (quote not in the note, value not in the quote, implausible 
          -or malformed) and no newer verified reading of the same type s
          -upersedes it. Then Rule 4 is INDETERMINATE and the case gets `
          -MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA`, so a nurse confirms the latest value. 
          -Before this rule, an unverifiable 157/117 silently fell back t
          -o an older normal reading and returned READY (the first `gpt-4
          -.1-mini` run). An unverified value is never used to issue NOT_
          -CLEARED either.                                               
      133 -- **Safety rule:** a rule that couldn't be evaluated can never
          - produce READY.                                               
      134 -                                                              
      135 -### 2. Starter baselines vs this pipeline: measured tokens, la
          -tency and cost                                                
      136 -                                                              
      137 -Each configuration was run on all 50 cases with 8 workers, usi
          -ng the API's `usage` fields. `python3 results/summarize_usage.
          -py` rebuilds this table from the saved files. Prices per 1M to
          -kens:                                                         
      138 -- `gpt-4.1-mini`: $0.40 input, $0.10 cached, $1.60 output (Ope
          -nAI's model page);                                            
      139 -- `gpt-5.6-luna`: $0.20 input, $0.02 cached, $1.20 output (thi
          -rd-party price trackers).                                     
      140 -                                                              
      141 -Reasoning tokens are billed as output.                        
      142 -                                                              
      143 -| Per case (mean) | Starter, `gpt-4.1-mini` | Starter, `gpt-5.
          -6-luna` (`reasoning: medium`) | Pipeline, `gpt-4.1-mini`, firs
          -t / repeat | **Pipeline, `gpt-5.6-luna`, first / repeat** |   
      144 -|---|---|---|---|---|                                         
      145 -| Input tokens | 3,039 | 3,029 | 3,830 | 3,828 |              
      146 -| Cached input tokens | 120 | 0 | 1,690 / 3,026 | 2,058 / 3,82
          -5 |                                                           
      147 -| Cache hit (by tokens) | 4.0% | 0.0% | 44.1% / 79.0% | **53.8
          -% / 99.9%** |                                                 
      148 -| Reasoning tokens | 0 | 145 | 0 | **0** |                    
      149 -| Visible = billed output tokens | 234 | 151 (297 billed) | 63
          -3 / 624 | 511 / 522 |                                         
      150 -| Latency per call, p50 | 2.08 s | 3.30 s | 4.31 / 4.15 s | 3.
          -77 / 3.39 s |                                                 
      151 -| Cost per 1,000 cases | $1.55 | $0.96 | $2.04 / $1.62 | **$1.
          -01 / $0.70** |                                                
      152 -                                                              
      153 -- **Against the repo default (`gpt-4.1-mini`):** the pipeline 
          -is 35% cheaper on first calls and 55% cheaper on repeats, and 
          -accuracy rises from 74.67% to 100%. It's slower per call (p50 
          -2.1 s → 3.4–3.8 s).                                           
      154 -- **Reasoning tokens are eliminated.** On `gpt-5.6-luna` the s
          -tarter spends 145 reasoning tokens per case applying the polic
          -y. The pipeline sends `reasoning.effort="none"`, and Python ap
          -plies the policy in about 45 ms per case.                     
      155 -- **Same pipeline on `gpt-4.1-mini`:** accuracy goes from 74.6
          -7% to 100%, but it costs more than its own starter: 32% more o
          -n first calls and 5% more on repeats. Its quotes are longer (a
          -bout 630 output tokens at $1.60 per 1M), it caches less, and i
          -t's less byte-stable (36 of 50 cases). Model choice drives cos
          -t and stability; the architecture drives accuracy.            
      156 -- **Visible output is larger, deliberately.** Verbatim quotes 
          -for every fact mean 511–522 visible tokens per case, against 1
          -51–234. That's the price of having Python check each fact agai
          -nst the chart instead of trusting the model. Caching more than
          - pays for it.                                                 
      157 -- **The cacheable prefix sits above OpenAI's 1,024-token minim
          -um.** System instructions plus the strict schema come to about
          - 2,100 tokens and are byte-identical on every request. All cas
          -e-specific content, including the fallback flags, goes in the 
          -single user message after them. The starter's static prefix is
          - below the minimum, so it caches little (4.0%) or nothing.    
      158 -                                                              
      159 -Cache behaviour was confirmed with a controlled experiment on 
          -a never-before-seen prefix:                                   
      160 -                                                              
      161 -| Request | Input | Cached |                                  
      162 -|---|---|---|                                                 
      163 -| Case 3, first call | 4,343 | 0 |                            
      164 -| Case 3, repeated | 4,343 | 4,340 |                          
      165 -| Case 7, same prefix | 3,867 | 2,123 (exactly the shared stat
          -ic prefix) |                                                  
      166 -| Case 7, one character changed at the front of the instructio
          -ns | 3,868 | 0 |                                              
      167 -                                                              
      168 -### 3. Audit trail: built for pre-op nurse review             
      169 -                                                              
      170 -The audit trail is designed for the production workflow, where
          - a pre-op nurse reviews blocked cases and samples READY ones b
          -efore the schedule is confirmed. Every output carries `audit`,
          - which contains:                                              
      171 -- the policy and prompt versions;                             
      172 -- how procedure date and risk were resolved (source, raw value
          -, notes);                                                     
      173 -- a `rule_checks` entry for every rule: `PASS` / `FAIL` / `IND
          -ETERMINATE` / `NOT_APPLICABLE`, each with a summary, source ID
          -s and quotes;                                                 
      174 -- `excluded_evidence`, listing data that was ignored and why; 
      175 -- `warnings` and `informational_checks`.                      
      176 -                                                              
      177 -Quotes marked `verbatim: true` are whole lines copied from the
          - source document, so a nurse can search the chart for them dir
          -ectly. Quotes marked `verbatim: false` are renderings of struc
          -tured rows (lab, vital, medication-list entry). Issues reuse t
          -he evidence of the rule check that failed.                    
      178 -                                                              
      179 -- **Blocked case (case 05, NOT_CLEARED):** `R4.BLOOD_PRESSURE`
          - reports the most recent blood pressure, BP 157/117 mmHg at 20
          -26-03-03 09:05, which meets the DBP ≥ 110 exclusion. It quotes
          - the nursing note line `09:05  BP 157/117, Temp 36.7 °C, HR 69
          -, SpO2 99% RA`. The structured vitals looked normal, and the a
          -udit shows exactly which newer reading drove the block.       
      180 -- **READY case worth sampling (case 16):** `R4.BLOOD_PRESSURE`
          - passes on the `08:30` recheck (137/78) and lists the supersed
          -ed `08:06` reading of 189/112 with both source lines. `R1.SURG
          -ICAL_CONSENT` quotes the patient signature line and the proced
          -ure line. A reviewer sampling READY cases can check every rule
          - against its quoted line without opening the full chart.      
      181 -- **Follow-up case (case 18):** `R3.ANTICOAGULATION.APIXABAN` 
          -fails with the medication-list entry and the note line "warfar
          -in was stopped last month and replaced with Eliquis". Warfarin
          - is `NOT_APPLICABLE`, and the evidence shows that its plan pre
          -dates the switch.                                             
      182 -                                                              
      183 -### 4. Clinical workflow boundary                             
      184 -                                                              
      185 -This came out of discussions with the owner of an electrophysi
          -ology clinic. Items such as NPO/fasting instructions are about
          - adherence on the day of surgery: nursing staff confirm them a
          -t intake and on the day itself. They aren't criteria for wheth
          -er a case can be scheduled. So the scheduling gate covers only
          - the appendix policy (Rules 1–4); the pipeline adds no criteri
          -a of its own.                                                 
      186 -                                                              
      187 -NPO review is still useful to the nurse, so a deterministic sc
          -an of nursing intake notes records it in `audit.informational_
          -checks` with one of five statuses:                            
      188 -- `REVIEWED_WITH_PATIENT`                                     
      189 -- `INSTRUCTIONS_DOCUMENTED`                                   
      190 -- `NOT_REVIEWED`                                              
      191 -- `NOT_DOCUMENTED`                                            
      192 -- `NO_NURSING_INTAKE_NOTE`                                    
      193 -                                                              
      194 -These checks are marked `affects_decision: false`, live outsid
          -e `rule_checks`, and are covered by tests showing they never c
          -hange the decision or issues.                                 
      195 -                                                              
      196 -## Testing                                                    
      197 -                                                              
      198 -`make test` runs 173 tests offline in about 2 seconds:        
      199 -- **Rule-by-rule unit tests**, including window boundaries, si
          -gnature and procedure matching, brand/generic reconciliation, 
          -lab statuses, timezones, mixed °C/°F strings and the NPO infor
          -mational check.                                               
      200 -- **Failure-mode tests:** invented, near-miss, stitched and wr
          -ong-document quotes; degree-sign corruption; fail-closed vital
          -s; procedure synonyms and wrong-side consents; the optional AI
          - matcher staying informational.                               
      201 -- **API-settings tests:** strict schema, `temperature=0`, `sto
          -re=False`, a static cache prefix, the client's timeout and ret
          -ries, and telemetry parsing.                                  
      202 -- **Replays of recorded real extractions** (`tests/fixtures/`,
          - cases 16 and 25).                                            
      203 -- **Hypothesis property tests** (`tests/test_properties.py`), 
          -with cases built on arbitrary dates from 2000 to 2099:        
      204 -  - H&P, CBC and CMP windows hold exactly at 14 and 30 days;  
      205 -  - a BMP never satisfies a CMP requirement;                  
      206 -  - the latest verified BP ≥ 180/110 or temperature > 100.4 °F
          - always gives NOT_CLEARED with `ACUTE_SAFETY_EXCLUSION`;      
      207 -  - older abnormal readings never override newer ones;        
      208 -  - °C converts exactly;                                      
      209 -  - a rejected latest vital never resolves to READY;          
      210 -  - the decision always agrees with the issue categories.     
      211 -                                                              
      212 -A mutation check confirmed the suite catches eight deliberate 
          -bugs: each threshold moved by one, `<=` changed to `<` in the 
          -lab window, and a fail-open supersession rule. The check also 
          -showed the threshold properties needed boundary-biased inputs,
          - which they now have.                                         
      213 -                                                              
      214 -## Code map                                                   
      215 -                                                              
      216 -| File | Role |                                               
      217 -|---|---|                                                     
      218 -| `core.py` | entry point: shared OpenAI client (30 s timeout,
          - 2 retries), facility timezone, telemetry hook |              
      219 -| `extraction.py` | Layer 1: preprocessing, prompt and strict 
          -schema, model call, usage telemetry, verification |           
      220 -| `policy_engine.py` | Layer 2: rules, issues, explanation, au
          -dit trail, informational checks |                             
      221 -| `reference.py` | drug, lab and title reference data; date/ti
          -me, quote and procedure-matching helpers |                    
      222 -| `schemas.py` | input, output and audit models |             
      223 -| `run_consistency.py` | multi-run consistency and telemetry r
          -eport |                                                       
      224 -| `WRITEUP.md` | approach and design write-up |               
      225 -| `ai_procedure_matcher.py` | optional, off by default: AI sug
          -gestion for procedure names that fail deterministic matching (
          -informational only) |                                         
      226 -| `results/` | raw outputs, eval reports and usage for all fou
          -r configurations (see `results/README.md`); `summarize_usage.p
          -y` rebuilds the cost table |                                  
      227 -| `tests/` | offline tests (`test_properties.py` holds the Hyp
          -othesis properties); `tests/fixtures/` holds recorded real ext
          -ractions plus the script that records them |                  
      228 -| `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` | runtime and dev dependencies |
      229 -                                                              
      230 -## Known limits                                               
      231 -                                                              
      232 -- **Timezone:** same-day ordering of UTC structured vitals aga
          -inst note times needs `PREOP_FACILITY_TIMEZONE`. Without it th
          -e most severe reading is used.                                
      233 -- **Procedure names:** consent matching uses a small reviewed 
          -synonym table (CABG, TKA/THA, replacement ↔ arthroplasty, hern
          -iorrhaphy, …) and treats a left/right conflict as a mismatch. 
          -Synonyms outside the table fail closed into follow-up. The opt
          -ional AI matcher (`ai_procedure_matcher.py`, off by default) c
          -an only *suggest* a match for the nurse.                      
      234 -- **Locale:** dates are parsed as US `M/D/YYYY`, and the text 
          -patterns are English.                                         
      235 -- **Large charts:** one model call per case. A chart large eno
          -ugh to hit the output limit raises `ExtractionError` for manua
          -l review; there's no chunking.                                
      236 -- **Model drift:** `gpt-5.6-luna` has no dated snapshot. The p
          -rompt-version hash in every audit, the recorded fixtures and `
          -make consistency` are how drift would be detected.            
      237 -- **Runtime failures:** model refusals or truncated responses 
          -raise `ExtractionError` instead of producing a guessed decisio
          -n.                                                            
      238 -- **Version of the `gpt-5.6-luna` results:** they were produce
          -d by pipeline 2.0.0 and kept frozen. The later changes (fail-c
          -losed vitals, degree-sign normalisation, pre-procedure filteri
          -ng, synonyms and laterality) were checked to change no decisio
          -n or issue in those 50 outputs; only summary wording and `pipe
          -line_version` would differ. The `gpt-4.1-mini` pipeline result
          -s are from the current code (2.2.0).

  ⎿  Updated README.md (+8 -0)
      17  
      18  ## Approach and design decisions
      19  
      20 +### Architecture                                               
      21 +                                                               
      22  I split the system into a constrained extraction layer and a pu
          re Python policy engine so the model does only what it is good 
          at. One strict-schema call reads the chart and returns facts (v
          itals, medication statements, hold and resume instructions, con
          sent signatures), each with a verbatim quote. Everything that m
          ust be exact lives in Python: date math, recency sorting, unit 
          conversion, anticoagulant versus antiplatelet classification, a
          nd procedure matching. The model never sees the policy, so it h
          as nothing to improvise, and Python rejects any quote it cannot
           find in the source. Once the policy moved into code, reasoning
           tokens were pure cost, so I pair `temperature=0` with `reasoni
          ng="none"`. Instructions and schema form a byte-identical 2,100
          -token prefix, above the 1,024-token caching threshold and rout
          ed with a versioned `prompt_cache_key`, with every case-specifi
          c detail after it. Verbatim quotes cost more output tokens than
           a bare decision, but in exchange I get a 54% cache hit on firs
          t calls and 99.9% on repeats. And because Python snaps every qu
          ote to its full source line, the outputs were byte-identical on
           all 50 cases across repeated runs.
      23  
      24 +### Proving it on a smaller model                              
      25 +                                                               
      26  To prove the architecture, not the model, drives accuracy, I ra
          n the same pipeline on gpt-4.1-mini, the repository's default. 
          The starter code scores 74.67% there; the pipeline scored 96.0%
           on its first run. The misses mattered more than the score. The
           smaller model corrupted the degree symbol when serializing quo
          tes, so Python correctly rejected genuinely abnormal vitals, bu
          t Rule 4 then fell back to older normal readings and cleared th
          ose charts. I made the rule fail closed: if the latest vital is
           unverified or malformed and no newer verified reading supersed
          es it, the chart goes to nurse review as `MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA
          `, and an unverified value can never trigger an exclusion. With
           that rule and degree-sign normalization, gpt-4.1-mini reached 
          100%. An offline pytest suite with Hypothesis properties pins t
          hese invariants across arbitrary dates, and a mutation check co
          nfirmed it catches off-by-one thresholds and fail-open regressi
          ons.
      27  
      28 +### Built for nurse review                                     
      29 +                                                               
      30  The output is built for the pre-op nurse who has to trust it. E
          ach issue carries the failed rule, a plain summary, source IDs 
          and verbatim-flagged quotes. The audit ledger records every rul
          e that passed, how procedure date and risk were resolved, and e
          very excluded record with its reason, so a nurse can confirm a 
          blocked chart or spot-check a ready one in seconds. I also spok
          e with the owner of an electrophysiology clinic about where cas
          es fall apart, and the answer was day-of-surgery adherence: pat
          ients forgetting fasting instructions or anticoagulant hold win
          dows. Hold windows are already in the policy. NPO review is not
          , so I track it as an informational nurse check in the audit tr
          ail, with complete provenance, while the scheduling gate stays 
          strictly bound to the appendix policy.
      31

          tarter, gpt-5.6-luna | Pipeline, gpt-5.6-luna |

  /export and choose to save to a file. Before that file goes on GitHub, though,
  there's one serious problem.

  This conversation contains pieces of your OpenAI API key. Early on, when the
  key in .env was split across lines, the shell printed two of those lines as
  errors (./.env:3: command not found: …), and a later check I ran also printed
  the first 15 characters of those lines. Together that's roughly the last 100
  characters of the key, and they'll be in the export. Please do both of these:

  1. Rotate the key in the OpenAI dashboard regardless. It has been shown in
     this session, so treat it as exposed.
  2. Redact the exported file before committing it.

  After you've exported (adjust the filename to whatever /export saves):

    -e 's/(starts with \[)[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,}\]/\1REDACTED]/g' \
    -e 's/sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}/sk-[REDACTED]/g' \

  \[[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,}|sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}' docs/ai-session-transcript.txt ||
